=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\.env.example ===
# ============================================================================
# Forum Project — Environment Variables Template
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Copy this file to .env and fill in the secrets.
#
#   cp .env.example .env
#
# Generate strong secrets with:
#   node -e "console.log('SESSION_SECRET=' + require('crypto').randomBytes(48).toString('hex'))"
#   node -e "console.log('CSRF_SECRET='    + require('crypto').randomBytes(48).toString('hex'))"
#   node -e "console.log('DB_PASSWORD='    + require('crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('hex'))"
#
# NEVER commit .env to version control.
# ============================================================================

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Server
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
NODE_ENV=development
PORT=3000
HOST=localhost

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sessions
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Both secrets MUST be at least 32 characters long.
# The server refuses to start if either is missing or too short.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
SESSION_SECRET=
CSRF_SECRET=
SESSION_MAX_AGE_HOURS=24

# --- F04 Session Hardening ---
SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MIN=120
SESSION_ABSOLUTE_TIMEOUT_DAYS=7
SESSION_BIND_UA=true

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Database
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Relative in development, absolute in production (/app/data/forum.db).
#
# DB_PASSWORD (P21):
#   - If set, the SQLite database is encrypted with SQLCipher.
#   - If empty, the database is plaintext (development only).
#   - MUST be at least 16 characters in production.
#   - If you enable this on an existing plaintext DB, the server WILL FAIL
#     to open it. Backup and recreate:
#       npm run db:backup  (before enabling)
#       # then set DB_PASSWORD and re-run migrations
#       # then restore with: npm run db:restore ./data/backup.db
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
DB_PATH=./data/forum.db
DB_PASSWORD=

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# TLS / Certificates
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Paths used in development. In production the Dockerfile uses:
#   /app/certs/cert.pem
#   /app/certs/key.pem
# Certificates are auto-generated if missing.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CERT_PATH=./certs/cert.pem
KEY_PATH=./certs/key.pem
CERT_DAYS=365
CERT_CN=localhost
CERT_C=EG
CERT_ST=Cairo
CERT_L=Cairo
CERT_O=Forum

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Rate Limiting
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MIN=15
RATE_LIMIT_MAX=100
AUTH_RATE_LIMIT_MAX=50

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Request Body Limit
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
BODY_LIMIT=10kb

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Pagination
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE=10
MAX_PAGE_SIZE=50

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# SSO / OAuth
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
APP_BASE_URL=https://localhost:3000
OAUTH_STATE_TTL_SECONDS=600

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Google OIDC
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=
GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET=
GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI=https://localhost:3000/auth/google/callback

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# GitHub OAuth2
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
GITHUB_CLIENT_ID=
GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET=
GITHUB_REDIRECT_URI=https://localhost:3000/auth/github/callback

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Media / Image Upload
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
MEDIA_ROOT=./media
MEDIA_MAX_FILE_BYTES=5242880
MEDIA_MAX_WIDTH=4000
MEDIA_MAX_HEIGHT=4000
MEDIA_OUTPUT_FORMAT=webp
MEDIA_OUTPUT_QUALITY=85
MEDIA_RESIZE_MAX_WIDTH=1920
MEDIA_RESIZE_MAX_HEIGHT=1920
MEDIA_MAX_PER_USER=200

=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\docker-compose.yml ===
services:
  forum:
    build: .
    container_name: forum_app
    ports:
      - "3000:3000"
    volumes:
      - forum_data:/app/data
      - forum_certs:/app/certs
      - forum_media:/app/media
    env_file:
      - .env
    environment:
      - NODE_ENV=production
      - PORT=3000
      - HOST=0.0.0.0
      - DB_PATH=/app/data/forum.db
      - MEDIA_ROOT=/app/media
      - DB_PASSWORD=${DB_PASSWORD}
    restart: unless-stopped

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # F04: Resource limits (prevent DoS via resource exhaustion)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          cpus: '1.0'
          memory: 512M
          pids: 200
        reservations:
          memory: 128M

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # F04: Read-only root filesystem (immutable container)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Writes are only allowed to mounted volumes + /tmp (tmpfs).
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp:rw,noexec,nosuid,size=64m
      - /app/.npm:rw,noexec,nosuid,size=8m

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # F04: Logging limits (prevent disk exhaustion)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    logging:
      driver: json-file
      options:
        max-size: "10m"
        max-file: "3"

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Health check (Node.js-based, IPv6 + HTTPS safe)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    healthcheck:
      test:
        - CMD
        - node
        - -e
        - |
          const https = require('https');
          const req = https.get(
            { host: 'localhost', port: 3000, path: '/auth/providers', rejectUnauthorized: false },
            (r) => process.exit(r.statusCode < 500 ? 0 : 1)
          );
          req.on('error', () => process.exit(1));
          req.setTimeout(3000, () => { req.destroy(); process.exit(1); });
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 20s

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    # F04: Security hardening
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true

    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    cap_add:
      - CHOWN
      - SETUID
      - SETGID
      - DAC_OVERRIDE

    # Graceful shutdown: give the app 30s to drain in-flight requests
    stop_grace_period: 30s

volumes:
  forum_data:
  forum_certs:
  forum_media:


=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\Dockerfile ===
# ---------- Stage 1: builder ----------
FROM node:22-alpine AS builder

# SQLCipher (better-sqlite3-multiple-ciphers) needs these to compile.
RUN apk add --no-cache python3 make g++ openssl-dev

WORKDIR /app
COPY package*.json ./
RUN npm ci

# ---------- Stage 2: runtime ----------
FROM node:22-alpine AS runtime

RUN apk add --no-cache openssl vips su-exec

# Fixed UID/GID 1001 for the app user.
RUN addgroup -g 1001 -S app && adduser -u 1001 -S app -G app

WORKDIR /app

COPY --from=builder /app/node_modules ./node_modules
COPY package*.json ./
COPY src ./src
COPY public ./public
COPY scripts ./scripts
COPY server.js ./
COPY entrypoint.sh ./

RUN mkdir -p /app/data /app/certs /app/media && chown -R app:app /app

EXPOSE 3000

ENV NODE_ENV=production
ENV PORT=3000
ENV DB_PATH=/app/data/forum.db
ENV MEDIA_ROOT=/app/media

ENTRYPOINT ["sh", "entrypoint.sh"]
CMD ["node", "server.js"]


=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\docs\ARCHITECTURE.md ===

# Architecture

## Layering

```text
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Client (Browser)                                   │
│   - HTML / CSS / Vanilla JS                         │
│   - No frontend framework (per constraints)         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                       │ HTTPS
                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Express App (src/app.js)                           │
│                                                     │
│  Middleware chain (order matters):                  │
│    1. Security headers (CSP, HSTS, XFO)             │
│    2. Body parsers (10kb limit)                     │
│    3. cookie-session (signed)                       │
│    4. General rate limiter                          │
│    5. attachSession (DB-validated)                  │
│    6. csrfProtection                                │
│    7. Static files                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Routes (src/routes/*)                              │
│   - URL → Controller mapping                        │
│   - apply requireAuth / validators / rate limiters  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Controllers (src/controllers/*)                    │
│   - HTTP-specific logic                             │
│   - Input validation (via middleware)               │
│   - Call services/models                            │
│   - Format responses                                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Services & Models (src/auth/*, src/models/*)       │
│   - Business logic                                  │
│   - DB queries (prepared statements)                │
│   - Transactions                                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                       │
                       ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  SQLite (better-sqlite3)                            │
│   - WAL mode                                        │
│   - Foreign keys enforced                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Why each layer exists

| Layer | Responsibility | Never does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Middleware** | Cross-cutting concerns (auth, CSRF, rate limit) | Business logic |
| **Routes** | URL mapping + middleware composition | DB queries |
| **Controllers** | HTTP in → domain call → HTTP out | Direct SQL |
| **Services** | Policy + orchestration across models | HTTP knowledge |
| **Models** | Prepared statements + domain queries | Business policy |

## Key request flows

### Flow 1: User registers

```text
POST /register
  │
  ├─ authLimiter (max 50 per 15 min per IP)
  ├─ validate(validateRegister)
  │
  ▼
AuthController.register
  │
  ├─ userModel.findByEmail     → 409 if exists
  ├─ userModel.findByUsername  → 409 if exists
  ├─ hashPassword (bcrypt 12)
  ├─ userModel.create
  │
  ▼
201 { id, username }
```

### Flow 2: User logs in

```text
POST /login
  │
  ├─ authLimiter
  ├─ validate(validateLogin)
  │
  ▼
AuthController.login
  │
  ├─ userModel.findByUsername → 401 if not found
  ├─ verifyPassword (bcrypt.compare)
  ├─ sessionModel.create (UUID, TTL)
  ├─ req.session.sid = session.id
  │
  ▼
200 { id, username } + Set-Cookie: forum_session=[signed]
```

### Flow 3: SSO login via Google

```text
GET /auth/google
  │
  ├─ ensureEnabled (404 if not configured)
  ├─ authLimiter
  │
  ▼
ssoController.start
  │
  ├─ generateCodeVerifier, generateCodeChallenge (S256)
  ├─ generateState, generateNonce
  ├─ oauthStateModel.create(state, verifier, nonce, ...)
  │
  ▼
302 → Google authorization endpoint
      (accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth?...)

[user authorizes on Google]

GET /auth/google/callback?code=...&state=...
  │
  ├─ ensureEnabled
  ├─ authLimiter
  │
  ▼
ssoController.callback
  │
  ├─ oauthStateModel.consume(state) → row or 400
  ├─ check stored.provider === 'google'
  ├─ provider.exchangeCode({ code, codeVerifier, nonce })
  │    ├─ openid-client verifies ID token signature, iss, aud, exp, nonce
  │    └─ returns profile { provider, providerUserId, email, emailVerified, ... }
  ├─ identityService.resolveUser(profile, { linkUserId })
  │    ├─ if identity exists          → login
  │    ├─ if email matches + verified → link
  │    ├─ if email matches + unverified → 409
  │    └─ else create new user + identity
  ├─ sessionModel.create (UUID)
  ├─ req.session.sid = session.id
  │
  ▼
302 → redirect_to (or /)
```

### Flow 4: Protected resource

```text
POST /api/posts
  │
  ├─ Security headers
  ├─ Body parser (10kb)
  ├─ cookie-session
  ├─ General rate limiter
  ├─ attachSession     → req.user = { id, username, sessionId } or null
  ├─ csrfProtection    → 403 if token missing/invalid
  ├─ requireAuth       → 401 if req.user null
  ├─ validate(validateCreatePost) → 400 if invalid
  │
  ▼
PostsController.createPost
  │
  ├─ verify each categoryId exists
  ├─ postModel.create (transaction: insert post + link categories)
  │
  ▼
201 { id }
```

### Flow 5: Image upload (F03)

```text
POST /api/posts/:id/media
  │
  ├─ Security headers
  ├─ Body parser (skipped for multipart)
  ├─ cookie-session
  ├─ General rate limiter
  ├─ attachSession
  ├─ csrfProtection
  ├─ requireAuth
  ├─ uploadLimiter (20 req/min)
  ├─ mediaUploadGuard (415 if not multipart)
  │
  ▼
MediaController.uploadPostMedia
  │
  ├─ verify post exists + ownership
  ├─ verify per-user quota
  ├─ src/media/index.js → handleUpload(req)
  │    ├─ upload.js   (busboy streaming, size-bounded)
  │    ├─ detect.js   (magic bytes → reject non-image)
  │    ├─ process.js  (sharp: decode, dimension check, re-encode, strip EXIF)
  │    └─ storage.js  (SHA-256, atomic write, directory sharding)
  ├─ mediaModel.create
  │
  ▼
201 { id, url, width, height }
```

## Design decisions

### Why server-side sessions?

- Cookie carries only an opaque UUID.
- Logout revokes on the server instantly.
- Rotation on login prevents fixation.
- TTL enforced in DB, not trusted from the cookie.
- Cookies are signed (HMAC) via `cookie-session` so tampering fails.

### Why SQLite + migrations?

- Zero external dependencies for the academy environment.
- WAL mode gives concurrent reads without writer blocking.
- Migration runner in `src/database/schema.js` is idempotent: safe on every boot.
- `schema_migrations` table records applied migrations so reruns skip them.

#### Single source of truth

All database code lives under `src/database/` — nowhere else:

| File | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `src/database/db.js` | Singleton connection + PRAGMAs (`foreign_keys = ON`, `journal_mode = WAL`) |
| `src/database/schema.js` | Ordered list of migrations (001–013) + the runner |
| `src/database/seed.js` | Idempotent default data (4 categories) |

There is intentionally **no `database.js` at the project root**. A leftover
prototype from before the layering refactor was removed because it:

1. Opened a **second connection** on a different path
   (`__dirname/forum.db` instead of `./data/forum.db`), silently causing
   "my data disappeared" confusion.
2. Defined a **stale, incompatible schema** — no `sessions`, no `identities`,
   no `oauth_states`, no `media`, no `reports`, no `notifications`, no
   `activity_logs`, and a `likes` table with a `post_id` column instead of
   the unified `reactions(target_type, target_id, value)`.
3. Was **never copied into the Docker image**, proving the running app
   already lived without it.

**Rule going forward:** any schema change MUST be added as a new numbered
migration in `src/database/schema.js`. Never add `db.exec('CREATE TABLE ...')`
anywhere else in the codebase.

### Why `openid-client` v6?

- OpenID Certified implementation.
- Native PKCE + nonce + ID token verification.
- Uses `fetch` (no HTTP library mismatch).
- Discovers issuer metadata automatically (Google).

### Why not Passport?

- Passport strategies add a layer of indirection.
- For only two providers and fine-grained control over linking, `openid-client` + raw OAuth2 (GitHub) is simpler and more auditable.

### Why double-submit CSRF instead of a library?

- No extra dependency.
- Constant-time comparison.
- HMAC-signed cookie (can't be forged).
- Token bound to the session lifetime.

### Why raw OAuth2 for GitHub?

- GitHub does **not** support OIDC discovery.
- No ID token, so no nonce validation is possible — but PKCE + state still work.
- We fetch `/user` and `/user/emails` explicitly and treat the primary email as verified.

### Why decode + re-encode for uploads?

- Re-encoding writes a **brand-new bitmap** from raw pixel data.
- Any appended bytes, EXIF tricks, or polyglot payloads are destroyed.
- This is the single most important defense against RCE via images.
- `sharp` also strips EXIF automatically, protecting user privacy.

### Why SHA-256 addressed storage?

- Deduplication: identical uploads share one file.
- Integrity: verifiable on backup/restore.
- Path safety: hex only — no `../`, no shell metacharacters.
- Directory sharding (`ab/cd/`) prevents one directory with millions of entries.

=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\docs\DEMO-SCRIPT.md ===

# Live Demo Script (7 Minutes)

## Setup (Before you start)

Open two terminal windows:

- Terminal 1: Run `docker compose up -d` (Ensure it's healthy).
- Terminal 2: Keep it open for Docker commands.
- Browser 1: Normal window.
- Browser 2: Incognito (Private) window.

## Step 1: Admin Bootstrapping (1 min)

1. Go to `https://localhost:3000` in Browser 1.
2. Register: `admin1` / `admin@test.com` / `password123`.
3. Look at the top: `Welcome, admin1! (admin)`.
4. Scroll down: Point out the **Moderation Dashboard**.

## Step 2: Create Content & Media (1 min)

1. Create a post: "Hello World" in "General".
2. **Choose an image before publishing** → point out the new **file input**.
3. Publish. The image appears below the post (F03 demo).
4. Point out the **Edit**, **Delete**, and **Add Image** buttons (owner-only).

## Step 3: Change Password (30 sec — F04)

1. Click **Change Password** (new orange button next to Logout).
2. Enter `password123` as current and `newpassword123` as new.
3. Save. You stay logged in (session rotation shown in logs).
4. (Optional) Open the DB with the wrong key to prove encryption fails.

## Step 4: Notifications & SSO (1 min)

1. Open Browser 2 (Incognito).
2. Register: `user1` / `user1@test.com` / `password123`.
3. Comment on `admin1`'s post: "Nice post!".
4. Go back to Browser 1 (admin1).
5. Click the Bell icon (🔔). Point out the unread notification.

## Step 5: Report Content (30 sec — F05)

1. In Browser 2 (as user1), click **Report** on admin1's post.
2. Enter: "Spam content" → Submit.
3. In Browser 1 (as admin1), scroll to **Moderation Dashboard** → **Open Reports** shows the new report.
4. Click **Resolve**.

## Step 6: Optimistic Locking / Stale Update (1 min)

1. In Browser 1, click Edit on the post. Change the title. Click Save.
2. In Browser 2 (as user1), try to edit the same post via API:

   ```bash
   curl -k -X PUT https://localhost:3000/api/posts/1 \
     -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
     -H "x-csrf-token: <token>" \
     -b "forum_session=<cookie>" \
     -d '{"title":"Hijacked","content":"Nope","categoryIds":[1],"updatedAt":"<old>"}'
   ```

   Response: `409 Conflict`.
3. *Explanation*: "The `updated_at` timestamp changed → the server refuses to overwrite."

## Step 7: Docker Security Proof (30 sec)

```bash
docker compose exec forum whoami
# Output: app

docker compose exec forum sh -c "touch /app/test.txt"
# Output: touch: /app/test.txt: Read-only file system

docker compose exec forum id
# Output: uid=1001(app) gid=1001(app) groups=1001(app)
```

## Step 8: P21 — Database Encryption Proof (1 min)

### 8.1 Prove the DB is encrypted

In Terminal 2:

```bash
# Peek at the first bytes of the DB file — should NOT show SQLite header
docker compose exec forum sh -c "head -c 16 /app/data/forum.db | od -c"
# Output: random-looking bytes (SQLCipher header, not "SQLite format 3")
```

### 8.2 Prove wrong password fails

```bash
# Try to open the DB without the password
docker compose exec forum node -e "
  const Database = require('better-sqlite3-multiple-ciphers');
  const db = new Database('/app/data/forum.db');
  try {
    db.prepare('SELECT count(*) FROM users').get();
    console.log('OPENED — encryption is NOT working!');
  } catch (err) {
    console.log('REJECTED:', err.message);
  }
"
# Output: REJECTED: file is not a database (or similar)
```

### 8.3 Prove backup/restore works

```bash
docker compose exec forum npm run db:backup
# Output: ✅ Backup created: /app/data/backup-XXXX.db

docker compose exec forum ls -la /app/data/
# Shows forum.db + backup-XXXX.db + manifest
```

## Conclusion

**Total runtime**: ~7 minutes.

You now have a demonstration that covers:

- ✅ F01 (Base Forum + posts + comments)
- ✅ F02 (Google + GitHub SSO)
- ✅ F03 (Image upload UI + pipeline)
- ✅ F04 (Password change + session rotation)
- ✅ F05 (Report UI + moderation queue)
- ✅ F06 (Optimistic locking + notifications)
- ✅ **P21 (SQLCipher encryption + backup)**

# Entity-Relationship Diagram (ERD)

## Visual Diagram

```mermaid
erDiagram
    users ||--o{ sessions        : "has"
    users ||--o{ posts           : "authors"
    users ||--o{ comments        : "writes"
    users ||--o{ identities      : "links"
    users ||--o{ reactions       : "gives"
    users ||--o{ media           : "uploads"
    users ||--o{ reports         : "submits"
    users ||--o{ notifications   : "receives"
    users ||--o{ notifications   : "triggers (actor)"
    users ||--o{ activity_logs   : "performs"

    posts ||--o{ comments        : "contains"
    posts ||--o{ post_categories : "tagged"
    posts ||--o{ reactions       : "receives as post"
    posts ||--o{ media           : "attaches"
    posts ||--o{ reports         : "targeted by"
    posts ||--o{ notifications   : "targets as post"

    comments ||--o{ reactions    : "receives as comment"
    comments ||--o{ reports      : "targeted by"
    comments ||--o{ notifications: "targets as comment"

    categories ||--o{ post_categories : "applied"

    users {
        INTEGER id PK
        TEXT    username UK
        TEXT    email    UK
        TEXT    password "bcrypt or !unusable"
        TEXT    role     "user|moderator|admin"
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    sessions {
        TEXT    id PK "UUID"
        INTEGER user_id FK
        DATETIME expires_at
        DATETIME last_seen_at
        TEXT    user_agent_hash
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    identities {
        INTEGER id PK
        INTEGER user_id FK
        TEXT    provider "google|github"
        TEXT    provider_user_id
        TEXT    email
        TEXT    display_name
        TEXT    avatar_url
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    posts {
        INTEGER id PK
        TEXT    title
        TEXT    content
        INTEGER user_id FK
        TEXT    status   "approved|pending|rejected"
        DATETIME created_at
        DATETIME updated_at
    }

    comments {
        INTEGER id PK
        TEXT    content
        INTEGER user_id FK
        INTEGER post_id FK
        TEXT    status   "approved|pending|rejected"
        DATETIME created_at
        DATETIME updated_at
    }

    reports {
        INTEGER id PK
        INTEGER reporter_id FK
        TEXT    target_type "post|comment"
        INTEGER target_id
        TEXT    reason
        TEXT    status "open|resolved|dismissed"
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    notifications {
        INTEGER id PK
        INTEGER user_id FK "receiver"
        INTEGER actor_id FK "triggerer"
        TEXT    type "like|comment|reply"
        TEXT    target_type "post|comment"
        INTEGER target_id
        INTEGER is_read
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    activity_logs {
        INTEGER id PK
        INTEGER user_id FK
        TEXT    action "post_create|comment_create|post_edit|comment_edit"
        TEXT    target_type "post|comment"
        INTEGER target_id
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    reactions {
        INTEGER id PK
        INTEGER user_id FK
        TEXT    target_type "post|comment"
        INTEGER target_id
        INTEGER value "-1|1"
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    media {
        INTEGER id PK
        INTEGER user_id FK
        INTEGER post_id FK "nullable"
        TEXT    kind "post_image|avatar"
        TEXT    original_name
        TEXT    mime_type
        INTEGER size_bytes
        INTEGER width
        INTEGER height
        TEXT    storage_path
        TEXT    sha256
        DATETIME created_at
    }

    oauth_states {
        TEXT    state PK
        TEXT    code_verifier
        TEXT    nonce
        TEXT    provider
        TEXT    redirect_to
        INTEGER link_user_id FK "nullable"
        DATETIME expires_at
    }

    categories {
        INTEGER id PK
        TEXT    name UK
    }

    post_categories {
        INTEGER post_id PK,FK
        INTEGER category_id PK,FK
    }

    schema_migrations {
        INTEGER id PK
        TEXT    name UK
        DATETIME applied_at
    }
```

## Table-by-Table Explanations

### Core & Authentication
- **`users`**: Stores local accounts. The `role` column enables RBAC (User, Moderator, Admin). SSO-only users have a password starting with `!`, making local login impossible.
- **`sessions`**: Server-side session store. Includes `last_seen_at` for idle timeouts and `user_agent_hash` to prevent session hijacking (cookie theft).
- **`identities`**: Links external providers (Google, GitHub) to local users. Enables multiple SSO methods per account.
- **`oauth_states`**: Single-use, TTL-bounded store for OAuth `state`, `code_verifier` (PKCE), and `nonce`.

### Community Content
- **`posts` & `comments`**: Core content. The `status` column allows moderators to approve or reject content. The `updated_at` column enables optimistic locking (preventing stale edits).
- **`categories` & `post_categories`**: Many-to-many relationship for subforums.
- **`reactions`**: Unified table for likes/dislikes on both posts and comments.

### Moderation & Advanced Features (F05 & F06)
- **`reports`**: User-generated reports against posts or comments. Moderators can resolve or dismiss them.
- **`notifications`**: Triggered when a user likes or comments on someone else's content. Links an `actor` (who did it) to a `user` (who receives it).
- **`activity_logs`**: Tracks user actions (post creation, edits, comments) for the personal activity feed.

### Media
- **`media`**: Stores uploaded images. Paths are SHA-256 addressed (content-addressed storage). `post_id` is nullable to support standalone avatars.


=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\docs\MEDIA-PIPELINE.md ===

# Media Upload Pipeline

## Purpose

Every uploaded file passes through a **deterministic, multi-stage pipeline**
before it is stored. This prevents:

- Remote Code Execution (RCE) via crafted images
- Polyglot files (valid image + embedded script)
- MIME spoofing (`.exe` renamed to `.jpg`)
- Decompression bombs (small file, huge decoded bitmap)
- EXIF privacy leaks (GPS coordinates)
- Path traversal in storage
- DoS via unbounded uploads
- Stored XSS via SVG

## Pipeline stages

```text
Client POST (multipart/form-data)
    │
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. Streaming intake (busboy)                │
│    - Hard cap on total bytes (5 MB)         │
│    - Hard cap on number of parts (10)       │
│    - Hard cap on number of files (1)        │
│    - Hard cap on field sizes                │
│    - Reject extra fields                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │  Buffer (≤ 5 MB)
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 2. Magic-byte detection (file-type)         │
│    - Read first bytes of buffer             │
│    - Accept only: jpeg, png, webp, gif      │
│    - Reject: svg, pdf, exe, zip, everything │
│    - Ignore the client's Content-Type       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │  { ext, mime }
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 3. Decode + re-encode (sharp)               │
│    - sharp(buf).metadata() → width/height   │
│    - Reject if width > 4000 or height > 4000│
│    - sharp(buf).rotate().resize(...).webp() │
│    - EXIF stripped automatically            │
│    - Output: brand-new WebP buffer          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │  { buffer, width, height, mime: image/webp }
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 4. Storage (src/media/storage.js)           │
│    - SHA-256 of final buffer                │
│    - Path = <root>/<ab>/<cd>/<sha256>.<ext> │
│    - Write atomically (tmp + rename)        │
│    - Never trust original_name              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │  { storagePath, sha256, sizeBytes }
    ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 5. DB insert (media.model.js)               │
│    - Row: user_id, post_id, kind, mime,     │
│      size, width, height, storage_path,     │
│      sha256, original_name (display only)   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    │
    ▼
201 { id, url, width, height }
```

## Why re-encode?

Re-encoding with `sharp` writes a **brand-new file** from raw pixel data. Any
appended bytes, EXIF tricks, or polyglot payloads are destroyed. This is the
single most important security measure in the pipeline.

Consider a JPEG file that is:

```text
[valid JPEG bytes] [PHP webshell] [valid JPEG trailer]
```

A naive app that only checks magic bytes would accept it. If the file is ever
served with a PHP handler (misconfiguration, or moved to another server), the
webshell executes. By decoding to raw pixels and re-encoding, we produce a
clean file containing only the visible image.

## Why not trust Content-Type?

The client sends `Content-Type: image/jpeg` for any file. Attackers rename
`shell.php` to `shell.jpg` and set the header. Only magic bytes reveal the
truth, and only a full decode confirms it.

## Why WebP?

- Smaller files than JPEG/PNG at similar quality.
- Widely supported (all modern browsers).
- Configurable via `MEDIA_OUTPUT_FORMAT`.

## Why SHA-256 in the path?

- Deduplication: identical uploads map to one file.
- Integrity: verifiable on backup/restore.
- Path safety: hex only — no `../`, no shell metacharacters.
- Deterministic: the same bytes always produce the same path.

## Directory sharding

`<root>/ab/cd/abc...webp` — first 4 hex chars as two 2-char directories.
Prevents a single directory with millions of entries on large deployments.
Theoretically, 16^4 = 65,536 shard directories, each with a manageable number
of files even at millions of uploads.

## Limits enforced

| Limit | Default | Configurable | Enforced by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Max file size | 5 MB | `MEDIA_MAX_FILE_BYTES` | busboy streaming |
| Max files per request | 1 | hard-coded | busboy |
| Max fields per request | 5 | hard-coded | busboy |
| Max parts per request | 10 | hard-coded | busboy |
| Max decoded width | 4000 | `MEDIA_MAX_WIDTH` | sharp metadata |
| Max decoded height | 4000 | `MEDIA_MAX_HEIGHT` | sharp metadata |
| Output resize cap (W) | 1920 | `MEDIA_RESIZE_MAX_WIDTH` | sharp resize |
| Output resize cap (H) | 1920 | `MEDIA_RESIZE_MAX_HEIGHT` | sharp resize |
| Output format | webp | `MEDIA_OUTPUT_FORMAT` | sharp |
| Output quality | 85 | `MEDIA_OUTPUT_QUALITY` | sharp |
| Per-user quota | 200 | `MEDIA_MAX_PER_USER` | media.model count |
| Upload rate | 20/min | hard-coded | express-rate-limit |

## Error responses

| Condition | Status | Message |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Wrong Content-Type | 415 | Expected multipart/form-data |
| No file provided | 400 | No file provided |
| File too large (during stream) | 413 | File too large |
| Too many files | 400 | Too many files |
| Too many fields | 400 | Too many fields |
| Magic bytes not allowed | 415 | Unsupported file type. Allowed: JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF. |
| Corrupted image | 400 | Invalid or corrupted image |
| Dimensions exceed limit | 413 | Image too large. Max 4000x4000. |
| Post not found | 404 | Post not found |
| Not post owner | 403 | You can only attach media to your own posts |
| Quota exceeded | 429 | Media quota exceeded |

## Lifecycle

### Upload

1. Authenticated user POSTs a file to `/api/posts/:postId/media`.
2. Middleware chain: `requireAuth` → `uploadLimiter` → `mediaUploadGuard` → controller.
3. Controller verifies post exists and belongs to user, quota not exceeded.
4. `handleUpload(req)` runs the full pipeline.
5. Row inserted in `media`, response returned with `{ id, url, width, height }`.

### Serve

1. Public `GET /api/media/:id`.
2. Look up row, resolve absolute path, verify file exists.
3. Stream with `Content-Type` from DB and `nosniff` header.
4. `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`.

### Delete

1. Authenticated owner `DELETE /api/media/:id`.
2. Verify `media.user_id === req.user.id`.
3. Delete DB row + delete file (ENOENT tolerated).

### Cascade

- Deleting a post deletes its `media` rows (SQLite `ON DELETE CASCADE`).
- Deleting a user deletes their `media` rows.
- Orphan files (no DB row) can be cleaned via `npm run media:cleanup`.

## Files involved

| File | Role |
| --- | --- |
| `src/media/upload.js` | busboy streaming intake |
| `src/media/detect.js` | magic-byte allow-list |
| `src/media/process.js` | decode + re-encode |
| `src/media/storage.js` | SHA-256 + atomic write |
| `src/media/index.js` | unified entry point |
| `src/models/media.model.js` | DB CRUD |
| `src/controllers/media.controller.js` | HTTP layer |
| `src/routes/media.routes.js` | URL mapping |
| `src/middleware/mediaUpload.js` | Content-Type guard (415) |
| `scripts/cleanup-media.js` | orphan cleanup |
| `scripts/seed-demo-media.js` | demo image seeding |
| `tests/media.*.test.js` | 3 test suites |

# Presentation Notes & Q&A

## Key Talking Points

### Introduction

"Today I will present my Forum Project, which covers the complete Academy Roadmap from F01 to F06. I didn't just build a standard forum; I built a hardened, production-ready application with 107 passing tests, zero security vulnerabilities, and SQLCipher database encryption at rest."

### Architecture

"I chose a layered architecture. When I write a controller, I only care about HTTP. When I write a model, I only care about SQL. This makes testing easier and the code cleaner."

### F02 (SSO)

"During SSO, the biggest risk is Account Takeover. If I login with Google using an email that already exists locally, the system checks if the Google email is verified. If not, it rejects the login. This stops attackers."

### F03 (Media Pipeline)

"File uploads are a massive attack vector. We use `sharp` to decode and re-encode the image. This destroys any embedded PHP or JS payloads (polyglots). We also strip EXIF data for privacy."

### F04 (Security & Docker)

"Sessions are protected with User-Agent binding. If a hacker steals a cookie and uses it on another browser, the session is instantly revoked. For Docker, I dropped all Linux capabilities (`cap_drop: ALL`) and made the root filesystem read-only. The app runs as a non-root user."

### F06 (Advanced)

"To prevent the 'Lost Update' problem, I implemented Optimistic Locking. When editing, the server checks the `updated_at` timestamp. If someone else changed it in the meantime, the server returns a 409 Conflict."

### P21 (Live DB Encryption)

"Beyond the roadmap, I implemented P21: the SQLite database is encrypted at rest with SQLCipher. This means if an attacker steals the `.db` file, they cannot read a single row without the `DB_PASSWORD`. I also built `db:backup` and `db:restore` scripts, and a dedicated test suite that proves a wrong password is rejected at open time."

---

## Top 10 Q&A

**1. Why did you use SQLite instead of PostgreSQL?**
"SQLite is the requirement. It's perfect for embedded databases. With WAL mode enabled, it handles concurrent reads well. Plus, it simplifies Docker deployment."

**2. How did you handle CSRF?**
"I used the Double-Submit Cookie pattern. A token is signed with HMAC-SHA256 and sent in a cookie. Unsafe methods (POST/PUT/DELETE) must return this token in a header. We use `timingSafeEqual` to compare them."

**3. How do you know a file is actually an image?**
"We never trust the file extension or the `Content-Type` header. We use `file-type` to check the magic bytes. Then we pass it to `sharp` to decode it. If it's not a real image, `sharp` throws an error."

**4. How are you handling password security?**
"We use `bcrypt` with a cost factor of 12. For SSO-only users, their password is set to a random string starting with `!`, which makes local login impossible. Passwords are never logged or returned in API responses."

**5. How do you handle database migrations?**
"I built a custom migration runner. Each migration is a numbered script. We have a `schema_migrations` table. When the server starts, it runs any pending migrations inside a transaction."

**6. Why did you add `su-exec` to the Dockerfile?**
"Because Docker volumes are created as root. The entrypoint script runs as root to fix volume permissions, then uses `su-exec` to drop privileges and run the Node.js app as UID 1001."

**7. How does the first user become admin?**
"In the `register` controller, we check `userModel.countAll()`. If it's `0`, we assign the `role` as `admin`. This is a bootstrapping mechanism."

**8. What happens if I try to edit a post after another user edited it?**
"The server returns a `409 Conflict`. We use the `updated_at` column. The client sends the timestamp they loaded. The SQL update includes `WHERE updated_at = ?`. If it doesn't match, the update fails."

**9. Why use `busboy` instead of `multer`?**
"`busboy` streams the file. `multer` often buffers the whole file into memory by default. With a 5MB limit, streaming is much safer and prevents memory exhaustion DoS attacks."

**10. Are there any frontend frameworks?**
"No. The Roadmap strictly forbids React, Vue, etc. I built the UI using Vanilla JavaScript and DOM manipulation. I also wrote a custom `escapeHtml` function to prevent XSS."



=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\docs\SECURITY.md ===
# Security & Threat Model

This document outlines the security posture of the Forum Project (P01–P26, F01–F06). It details the threat model, the mitigations implemented at each layer, and the architectural decisions made to ensure a production-ready application.

## Threat Model

**In Scope:**

- Network attackers (MitM, TLS stripping)
- CSRF/XSS attackers
- Credential attackers (Brute-force, Credential Stuffing)
- Session attackers (Hijacking, Fixation)
- Account Takeover (via SSO email mismatch)
- Malicious file uploads (RCE, Polyglots, Decompression bombs)
- Race Conditions (Stale updates)
- Privilege Escalation (Unauthorized moderation actions)
- DoS (Request floods, Slowloris, Body bombs)
- **Data at rest attackers (stolen DB file, backup tape)**

**Out of Scope:**

- Physical access to the host machine while running
- Compromised dependencies (mitigated by lockfiles and `npm audit`)
- Side-channel attacks (e.g., Spectre)

---

## 1. Authentication & Passwords (F01, F02)

### 1.1 Password Storage

- **bcrypt (cost 12)**: Passwords hashed using `bcryptjs`. Mitigates brute-force if DB leaks.
- **SSO-Only Users**: Password field set to unusable hash (starting with `!`). bcrypt fails on them.

### 1.2 SSO Account Linking (F02)

- **OIDC & OAuth2**: Google OIDC + PKCE + Nonce; GitHub raw OAuth2 + PKCE.
- **State Parameter**: Server-side SQLite, single-use, 10-minute TTL.
- **Account Takeover Defense**: Only link on `email_verified = true`. Unverified emails → 409.

---

## 2. Session Management (F04)

### 2.1 Session Architecture

- **Server-Side Store**: Cookie contains only an opaque, signed UUID.
- **Cookie Flags**: `HttpOnly`, `Secure` (in prod), `SameSite=Lax`.
- **User-Agent Binding**: UA hash stored; mismatch → revoke.

### 2.2 Session Lifecycle & Defense

- **Fixation**: On every login, all existing sessions deleted (`deleteAllForUser`).
- **Idle Timeout**: 2 hours.
- **Absolute Timeout**: 7 days.
- **Rotation**: `changePassword` issues a new session ID.

---

## 3. Access Control & Moderation (F05)

### 3.1 Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)

- `user`: Create content, react, report.
- `moderator`: Access queue, approve/reject, resolve reports.
- `admin`: Full access, change roles, create categories.

### 3.2 Route Guards

- `requireModerator`, `requireAdmin` at routing layer. Default deny (401/403).

### 3.3 Admin Bootstrapping

- First user in a fresh DB becomes `admin`.

### 3.4 Moderation Workflow

- **Approval Queue**: `pending` posts/comments hidden until approved.
- **Reports**: Users report content; moderators resolve/dismiss.

---

## 4. Data Integrity & Advanced Features (F06)

### 4.1 Optimistic Locking

- Client sends `updated_at`; SQL `WHERE updated_at = ?`; mismatch → **409 Conflict**.

### 4.2 Notifications & Privacy

- Bound to `user_id`. Self-actions don't notify.

---

## 5. Input Validation & Injection

### 5.1 SQL Injection (SQLi)

- 100% prepared statements.

### 5.2 Cross-Site Scripting (XSS)

- Custom `escapeHtml()` on frontend. Strict CSP.

### 5.3 CSRF

- Double-Submit Cookie + HMAC-SHA256. `timingSafeEqual`.

### 5.4 File Uploads (F03)

- Streaming bounds (5MB).
- Magic bytes via `file-type`.
- Re-encode via `sharp` (destroys polyglots, strips EXIF).

---

## 6. Infrastructure & Deployment (F04)

### 6.1 Docker Hardening

- **Non-Root**: UID 1001 (`app`). `su-exec`.
- **Read-Only FS**: `read_only: true`.
- **Resource Limits**: CPU 1.0, Memory 512MB, PIDs 200.
- **Capability Dropping**: `cap_drop: ALL` + 4 re-added.

### 6.2 Network & TLS

- **HTTPS Only**: `https.createServer`.
- **TLS 1.2/1.3 Only**: Mozilla Intermediate.
- **HSTS**: `max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload`.
- **Slowloris Defense**: Tight timeouts.

### 6.3 Rate Limiting

- IPv6-safe custom key generators.
- Layered: Login (10/15min/username), General (100/15min/IP), Content (10/5min, 20/5min), Uploads (10/min).

---

## 7. Data-at-Rest Encryption (P21 — Bonus)

### 7.1 Encryption

- **SQLCipher** via `better-sqlite3-multiple-ciphers`.
- **Key management**: `DB_PASSWORD` in `.env`. Must be ≥ 16 chars in production.
- **First operation**: `PRAGMA key` is applied before any query.

### 7.2 Failure modes (by design)

- Wrong/missing password → DB open fails at startup with clear error.
- Enabling `DB_PASSWORD` on an existing plaintext DB → fails fast, no data loss.
- Backup without password → impossible (script refuses).

### 7.3 Backup

- `npm run db:backup` → encrypted backup with same password + manifest.
- `ATTACH ... KEY` + `sqlcipher_export()` — atomic and portable.

### 7.4 Restore

- `npm run db:restore <path>` — verifies key, replaces DB, removes WAL/SHM.

### 7.5 Threat coverage

- **Stolen DB file / backup**: Without the password, the file is unreadable.
- **Accidental commit**: `.gitignore` excludes `data/` and `*.db`.
- **Forensics**: Plaintext never touches disk; only encrypted pages are written.

### 7.6 Limitations (honest)

- **Server-side key**: The password lives in `.env` on the server. If the host is compromised while running, the DB can be read via the live app. (Out of scope.)
- **No HSM integration**: The password is a plain env var, not an HSM/KMS secret.
- **Backup password = DB password**: Same secret for both. Rotating it requires both backup and DB re-encryption.
- **No key rotation UI**: Rotate via `cipher_rekey` manually (see below).

### 7.7 Key rotation (manual, advanced)

```bash
# Stop the server, then:
docker compose exec forum node -e "
  const Database = require('better-sqlite3-multiple-ciphers');
  const db = new Database('/app/data/forum.db');
  db.pragma(\"key = 'OLD_PASSWORD'\");
  db.pragma(\"rekey = 'NEW_PASSWORD'\");
  db.close();
"
# Update DB_PASSWORD in .env, restart.

# Forum Project: Full Stack Community

## From Authentication to Advanced Features (F01 → F06 + P21)

Prepared by:  الجنرال ياسر
Academy Roadmap Final Project

---

## The Journey (F01 → F06 + P21)

- **F01**: Base Forum (Auth, Posts, Comments, Reactions)
- **F02**: SSO Authentication (Google & GitHub with PKCE)
- **F03**: Verified Media Upload (Sharp, Busboy, Magic Bytes)
- **F04**: Security Hardening (Sessions, Rate Limits, Docker)
- **F05**: Moderation (Roles, Approval Queue, Reports)
- **F06**: Advanced Features (Editing, Notifications, Activity)
- **P21**: Live Database Encryption (SQLCipher Bonus)

**Result**: 18 Test Suites | 107 Tests | 0 Vulnerabilities

---

## Architecture: Layered & Modular

We used a strict Layered Architecture:

1. **Routes**: URL mapping & Middleware attachment.
2. **Middleware**: Auth, CSRF, Rate Limiting.
3. **Controllers**: HTTP request/response handling.
4. **Services & Models**: Business logic & Prepared SQL Statements.
5. **Database**: SQLite with WAL mode (and SQLCipher encryption).

*Why?* Separation of concerns, testability, and maintainability.

---

## F01 & F02: Secure Authentication

- **Local Auth**: bcrypt (cost 12) + Server-side Sessions (UUID).
- **Google SSO**: OpenID Connect (OIDC) with PKCE (S256).
- **GitHub SSO**: OAuth2 with raw PKCE.
- **Account Takeover Defense**: We **strictly reject** linking accounts if the provider returns an unverified email (`email_verified = false`).

---

## F03: Verified Media Pipeline

Uploading files is dangerous. We built a 4-stage pipeline:

1. **Busboy**: Streaming intake (limits memory).
2. **File-type**: Magic byte detection (no MIME spoofing).
3. **Sharp**: Decodes & re-encodes to WebP. Destroys polyglots, strips EXIF.
4. **Storage**: Atomic write, SHA-256 addressed, directory sharding.

---

## F04: Session & Infrastructure Security

- **Session Rotation**: New UUID on login. Kills fixation.
- **Global Logout**: Logging in kills all previous sessions (Single-session policy).
- **UA Binding**: Session tied to browser User-Agent hash.
- **Docker Hardening**: Read-only FS, `cap_drop: ALL`, Resource limits.
- **TLS 1.3**: Mozilla Intermediate ciphers, HSTS preload.

---

## F05: Moderation & RBAC

- **Roles**: User, Moderator, Admin.
- **Bootstrapping**: First registered user automatically becomes Admin.
- **Approval Queue**: Moderators can approve/reject pending posts/comments.
- **Reports**: Users report content; Moderators resolve/dismiss.

---

## F06: Advanced Features

- **Optimistic Locking**: Prevents "Stale Updates" (409 Conflict) using `updated_at` timestamps.
- **Notifications**: Bell icon triggered by likes/comments on your content.
- **Activity Feed**: Tracks personal history of edits and creations.

---

## P21: Live Database Encryption (Bonus)

- **SQLCipher**: `better-sqlite3-multiple-ciphers`.
- **Password-driven**: `DB_PASSWORD` in `.env` enables encryption.
- **Backup**: `npm run db:backup` → encrypted backup + manifest.
- **Restore**: `npm run db:restore <path>` → verified restore.
- **Verified**: `tests/f04.db-encryption.test.js` proves wrong passwords fail.

---

## Quality Assurance

- **18 Test Suites** / **107 Tests** (Jest + Supertest).
- **In-Memory DB**: Tests run isolated in `:memory:` SQLite.
- **Encrypted DB**: Tests use a temp file with SQLCipher (P21).
- **Security Check**: `npm audit` returns 0 vulnerabilities.

---

## Live Demonstration

### Switch to Browser

1. Admin Bootstrapping & Dashboard.
2. Creating posts & Stale Update protection.
3. Notifications & Activity Feed.
4. Docker read-only proof & Encrypted DB verification.

---

## Conclusion & What We Learned

- Building a **production-ready** app requires more than just CRUD.
- Security is a **layered defense** (Session, Data, Infrastructure).
- **Testing** is what gives us confidence to refactor and scale.

*Thank you! Questions?*



=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\entrypoint.sh ===
#!/bin/sh
set -e

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Ensure directories exist
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
mkdir -p /app/data /app/certs /app/media

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Fix volume ownership (Docker volumes are created as root by default)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
chown -R 1001:1001 /app/data /app/certs /app/media

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. Generate self-signed certificates if missing
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
if [ ! -f /app/certs/cert.pem ] || [ ! -f /app/certs/key.pem ]; then
  echo "[entrypoint] Generating self-signed certificates..."
  openssl req -nodes -new -x509 \
    -keyout /app/certs/key.pem \
    -out /app/certs/cert.pem \
    -days "${CERT_DAYS:-365}" \
    -subj "/C=${CERT_C:-EG}/ST=${CERT_ST:-Cairo}/L=${CERT_L:-Cairo}/O=${CERT_O:-Forum}/CN=${CERT_CN:-localhost}"
  chown 1001:1001 /app/certs/key.pem /app/certs/cert.pem
fi

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 4. Drop privileges to UID 1001 and run the app
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Using numeric UID:GID avoids getpwnam lookup that can fail on Alpine.
exec su-exec 1001:1001 "$@"


=== File: C:\dev\forum-project\generate-certs.js ===
// ============================================================================
// generate-certs.js — Local helper to create a self-signed TLS pair
// ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// NOTE: In Docker, entrypoint.sh handles certificate generation via openssl.
// This script is only for local development when you want explicit control.
//
// selfsigned@5 returns a Promise — the call MUST be awaited.
//
// Usage: node generate-certs.js
// ============================================================================

const fs = require('fs');
const selfsigned = require('selfsigned');

async function main() {
  const attrs = [{ name: 'commonName', value: 'localhost' }];

  const pems = await selfsigned.generate(attrs, {
    days: 365,
    keySize: 2048,
    algorithm: 'sha256',
  });

  if (!pems || !pems.private || !pems.cert) {
    throw new Error('selfsigned returned an unexpected shape');
  }

  fs.writeFileSync('key.pem', pems.private, { mode: 0o600 });
  fs.writeFileSync('cert.pem', pems.cert, { mode: 0o644 });

  console.log('Certificates generated successfully:');
  console.log('  key.pem   (mode 0600)');
  console.log('  cert.pem  (mode 0644)');
}

main().catch((err) => {
  console.error('Failed to generate certificates:', err);
  process.exit(1);
});
